\end{overpic}

Figure 6
:
Notation around a square face
y=\{e,e^{*}\}
, seen from the primal edge
e
point of view (left), or from the dual edge
e^{*}
point of view (right). Observe that we have
\tilde{\alpha}_{e}=\beta_{e},\tilde{\beta}_{e}=\alpha_{e}^{\prime}=\alpha_{e}+\rho
.

By Lemma
22
, face-weights are meromorphic functions of the parameters. Therefore we can choose all the non-prime angles
\alpha
to have fixed values in
{\mathbb{C}}
, and all prime angles to satisfy
\alpha^{\prime}=\alpha+\rho
, where
\alpha
can be any of the angles
\alpha,\alpha_{i},\tilde{\alpha}_{i}
above. Note that this choice
fixes the values in
{\mathbb{C}}
of the angles
(\beta_{i}),(\tilde{\beta}_{i})
, and we have

\beta_{i+1}=\alpha_{i}^{\prime}=\alpha_{i}+\rho,\quad\tilde{\beta}_{i+1}=\tilde{\alpha}_{i}^{\prime}=\tilde{\alpha}_{i}+\rho.

(38)

Using further Remark
23
, we know that face-weights are also meromorphic functions of the value of the discrete Abel map at the vertex corresponding to the face. It is thus useful to compute values of the discrete Abel map
\mathbf{d}
in
{\mathbb{T}}(\tau)
. Let us choose a vertex
v_{0}
of
G
and set
\mathbf{d}(v_{0})=0
. Note that this is consistent with Necessary Conditions I for the possible range of the angle map
\bm{\alpha}
, and this is not restrictive since we have not yet fixed the value of the parameter
t
.

Lemma 35
.

Values of the discrete Abel map in
{\mathbb{T}}(\tau)
are given by:

\displaystyle\mathbf{d}(x)=\begin{cases}0&\text{ if $x$ is a primal vertex $v$},\\
\rho&\text{ if $x$ is a dual vertex $f$},\\
(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})+\rho&\text{ if $x$ is a square face $y=\{e,e^{*}\}$ of ${\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}$, with the notation of Figure\penalty\ \ref{fig:square_face}}.\end{cases}

(39)

Proof.

Using the notation of Figure
6
(left), we have:

\displaystyle\mathbf{d}(v_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{d}(v_{1})+\alpha_{e}-\alpha^{\prime}_{e}+\beta_{e}-\beta^{\prime}_{e}=\mathbf{d}(v_{1})-2\rho\ [\Lambda]=\mathbf{d}(v_{1})\ [\Lambda]

\displaystyle\mathbf{d}(f_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{d}(f_{1})+\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}-(\beta^{\prime}_{e}-\alpha^{\prime}_{e})=\mathbf{d}(f_{1})\ [\Lambda]

\displaystyle\mathbf{d}(f_{1})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{d}(v_{1})-\alpha_{e}^{\prime}+\alpha_{e}=\mathbf{d}(v_{1})-\rho\ [\Lambda]

\displaystyle\mathbf{d}(y)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{d}(v_{1})+\beta_{e}-\alpha^{\prime}_{e}=\mathbf{d}(v_{1})+(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})-\rho\ [\Lambda].

The proof is concluded by using that
\mathbf{d}(v_{0})=0
.
∎

We now turn to the computation of face-weights.

Lemma 36
.

Consider Fock’s dimer model on an infinite, minimal graph
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
with parameters
\tau,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t
satisfying Necessary Conditions II. Then, the face-weights are explicitly given by, for every square face
y
, for every face corresponding to a dual vertex
f
, resp. primal vertex
v
:

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(y)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{j+j\ell}\frac{\theta_{1,1}^{2}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})}{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}^{2}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})}\frac{\theta_{0,0}^{2}(t)}{\theta_{\ell,j}^{2}(t)}=(-1)^{j+j\ell}\frac{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}^{2}(\tilde{\beta}_{e}-\tilde{\alpha}_{e})}{\theta_{1,1}^{2}(\tilde{\beta}_{e}-\tilde{\alpha}_{e})}\frac{\theta_{0,0}^{2}(t)}{\theta_{\ell,j}^{2}(t)}

(40)

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(f)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|f|}\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}\frac{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}(0)}{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i})}\frac{\theta_{0,0}(t+\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i})}{\theta_{0,0}(t)}

(41)

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(v)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|v|}\prod_{i=1}^{|v|}\frac{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}(\tilde{\alpha}_{i}-\tilde{\alpha}_{i-1})}{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}(0)}\frac{\theta_{\ell,j}(t)}{\theta_{\ell,j}(t+\tilde{\alpha}_{i}-\tilde{\alpha}_{i-1})}.

(42)

In particular the model is non-degenerate if
t\notin\{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2},\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2}+\rho\}\ [\Lambda]
.

Proof.

The fact that the model is non degenerate if
t\notin\{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2},\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2}+\rho\}\ [\Lambda]
comes from looking at the face-weight at a square face, recalling that the zeros of
\theta_{(0,0)}(\,\cdot\,)
are located at
\bigl(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2}\bigr)\ [\Lambda]
, using Identity (
22
), and observing that if
t\in\{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2},\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2}+\rho\}\ [\Lambda]
, the face-weight is either equal to
0
or to infinity, which gives a degenerate model.

In the three following computations, we use Equation (
29
) to compute the face-weight with the relevant choice of labeling of the angles, as given in Figures
5
and
6
. We choose the value of the angle map
\mathbf{d}
at the center vertex according to Lemma
35
, with a lift that suits our computations, and we use Equation (
38
).
We also need the following two identities, which can readily obtained from Identities (
22
) and (
23
). For all
x,y\in{\mathbb{C}}
, we have

\frac{\theta_{m,n}(x+\rho)}{\theta_{m,n}(y+\rho)}=e^{-i\pi\ell(x-y)}\frac{\theta_{m+\ell,n+j}(x)}{\theta_{m+\ell,n+j}(y)},\quad\frac{\theta_{m,n}(x-\rho)}{\theta_{m,n}(y-\rho)}=e^{i\pi\ell(x-y)}\frac{\theta_{m+\ell,n+j}(x)}{\theta_{m+\ell,n+j}(y)}.

(43)

Square face
y=\{e,e^{*}\}
.
We choose
\mathbf{d}(y)
to be equal to
(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})+\rho
, then we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(y)

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta_{1,1}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})\theta_{1,1}(\beta^{\prime}_{e}-\alpha^{\prime}_{e})}{\theta_{1,1}(\alpha^{\prime}_{e}-\beta_{e})\theta_{1,1}(\alpha_{e}-\beta^{\prime}_{e})}\frac{\theta_{0,0}(t+\mathbf{d}(y)+\alpha^{\prime}_{e}-\beta_{e})\theta_{0,0}(t+\mathbf{d}(y)+\alpha_{e}-\beta^{\prime}_{e})}{\theta_{0,0}(t+\mathbf{d}(y)+\alpha_{e}-\beta_{e})\theta_{0,0}(t+\mathbf{d}(y)+\alpha^{\prime}_{e}-\beta^{\prime}_{e})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta_{1,1}^{2}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})}{\theta_{1,1}(\alpha_{e}-\beta_{e}+\rho)\theta_{1,1}(\alpha_{e}-\beta_{e}-\rho)}\frac{\theta_{0,0}(t+2\rho)\theta_{0,0}(t)}{\theta_{0,0}^{2}(t+\rho)}.

Using Identities (
21
) and (
23
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(y)

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta_{1,1}^{2}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})}{q^{-\ell/2}i^{2(\ell+j\ell)}(-1)^{j\ell+\ell+j}\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}^{2}(\alpha_{e}-\beta_{e})}\frac{\theta_{0,0}^{2}(t)q^{-\ell}e^{-2i\pi\ell t}}{q^{-\ell/2}e^{-2i\pi\ell t}i^{2j\ell}\theta_{\ell,j}^{2}(t)}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{j+j\ell}\frac{\theta_{1,1}^{2}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})}{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}^{2}(\alpha_{e}-\beta_{e})}\frac{\theta_{0,0}^{2}(t)}{\theta_{\ell,j}^{2}(t)},

and the proof of the first identity is concluded by using that
\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}(\,\cdot\,)

is odd. Observing that
\tilde{\alpha}_{e}=\beta_{e},\tilde{\beta}_{e}=\alpha_{e}+\rho
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(y)=(-1)^{j+j\ell}\frac{\theta_{1,1}^{2}(\tilde{\alpha}_{e}-\tilde{\beta}_{e}+\rho)}{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}^{2}(\tilde{\alpha}_{e}-\tilde{\beta}_{e}+\rho)}\frac{\theta_{0,0}^{2}(t)}{\theta_{\ell,j}^{2}(t)}=(-1)^{j+j\ell}\frac{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}^{2}(\tilde{\alpha}_{e}-\tilde{\beta}_{e})}{\theta_{1,1}^{2}(\tilde{\alpha}_{e}-\tilde{\beta}_{e})}\frac{\theta_{0,0}^{2}(t)}{\theta_{\ell,j}^{2}(t)},

where in the last equality, we used Identity (
43
).

Face corresponding to a dual vertex
f
.
We choose
\mathbf{d}(f)=-\rho
, then we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(f)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}\frac{\theta_{1,1}(\alpha_{i}-\beta_{i+1})}{\theta_{1,1}(\beta_{i}-\alpha_{i})}\frac{\theta_{0,0}(t+\mathbf{d}(f)+\beta_{i}-\alpha_{i})}{\theta_{0,0}(t+\mathbf{d}(f)+\beta_{i+1}-\alpha_{i})}=\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}\frac{\theta_{1,1}(-\rho)}{\theta_{1,1}(\beta_{i}-\alpha_{i})}\frac{\theta_{0,0}(t-\rho+\beta_{i}-\alpha_{i})}{\theta_{0,0}(t)}

(44)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}(-1)\frac{\theta_{1,1}(\rho)}{\theta_{1,1}(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i}+\rho)}\frac{\theta_{0,0}(t+\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i})}{\theta_{0,0}(t)}.

Then using Identity (
43
), we obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(f)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{|f|}\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}e^{i\pi\ell(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i})}\frac{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}(0)}{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i})}\frac{\theta_{0,0}(t+\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i})}{\theta_{0,0}(t)}.

The proof is concluded by using that, since the angles
(\alpha_{i})
are fixed,

\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}e^{-i\pi\ell(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i})}=\penalty\ 1
.

Face corresponding to a primal vertex
v
.
We choose
\mathbf{d}(v)=0
, and obtain

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(v)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{|v|}\frac{\theta_{1,1}(\tilde{\beta}_{i}-\tilde{\alpha}_{i})}{\theta_{1,1}(\tilde{\alpha}_{i}-\tilde{\beta}_{i+1})}\frac{\theta_{0,0}(t+\mathbf{d}(v)+\tilde{\alpha}_{i}-\tilde{\beta}_{i+1})}{\theta_{0,0}(t+\mathbf{d}(v)+\tilde{\alpha}_{i}-\tilde{\beta}_{i})}=\prod_{i=1}^{|v|}\frac{\theta_{1,1}(\tilde{\beta}_{i}-\tilde{\alpha}_{i})}{\theta_{1,1}(-\rho)}\frac{\theta_{0,0}(t-\rho)}{\theta_{0,0}(t+\tilde{\alpha}_{i}-\tilde{\beta}_{i})}

(45)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{|v|}(-1)\frac{\theta_{1,1}(\tilde{\alpha}_{i-1}-\tilde{\alpha}_{i}+\rho)}{\theta_{1,1}(\rho)}\frac{\theta_{0,0}(t-\rho)}{\theta_{0,0}(t+\tilde{\alpha}_{i}-\tilde{\alpha}_{i-1}-\rho)}.

Using Identity (
43
) for both factors we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(v)=(-1)^{|v|}\prod_{i=1}^{|v|}e^{-i\pi\ell(\tilde{\alpha}_{i-1}-\tilde{\alpha}_{i})}e^{i\pi\ell(\tilde{\alpha}_{i}-\tilde{\alpha}_{i-1})}\frac{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}(\tilde{\alpha}_{i-1}-\tilde{\alpha}_{i})}{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}(0)}\frac{\theta_{\ell,j}(t)}{\theta_{\ell,j}(t+\tilde{\alpha}_{i}-\tilde{\alpha}_{i-1})}.

The exponential terms cancel because the
(\tilde{\alpha}_{i})
’s are fixed, and we have
\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}(\tilde{\alpha}_{i-1}-\tilde{\alpha}_{i})=\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}(\tilde{\alpha}_{i}-\tilde{\alpha}_{i-1})
because
(j,\ell)\neq(0,0)
so that the theta function is even.
∎

Duality.

As in Section
2.3.2
, suppose that the graph
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
is constructed from the dual graph
G^{*}
instead of
G
, while keeping the bipartite coloring fixed. Then, the graph
\mathsf{G}^{\diamond\scriptscriptstyle{/2}}
is unchanged, so that train-tracks and the angle map are the same as well. The only thing that gets modified in the construction of Fock’s dimer model is the base point of the discrete Abel map, which is now a primal vertex of the dual graph
G^{*}
, that is a dual vertex of the graph
G
. Denoting by
\mathscr{W}_{G,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(\,\cdot\,)
, resp.
\mathscr{W}_{G^{*},\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(\,\cdot\,)
, the face-weights of Fock’s dimer model on
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
arising from
G
, resp. from
G^{*}
, we prove the following.

Lemma 37
.

Face-weights of Fock’s dimer model on the graph
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
arising from
G
and from
G^{*}
are related by the following:

\mathscr{W}_{G,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(\,\cdot\,)=\mathscr{W}_{G^{*},\bm{\alpha},\rho,t+\rho}(\,\cdot\,).

Proof.

Changing the base point of the discrete Abel map from a primal vertex of
G
to a primal vertex of
G^{*}
has the effect of translating all values by
\rho
; indeed the discrete Abel map is defined additively, and by Lemma
35
, we know that
\mathbf{d}(v)-\mathbf{d}(f)=\rho
in
{\mathbb{T}}(\tau)
. The proof is concluded by recalling that the discrete Abel map is always summed with
t
in the face-weights, see Equation (
28
) and also Remark
23
.
∎

Remark 38
.

Since when constructing the graph
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
from the dual graph
G^{*}
, the bipartite coloring is not exchanged, our choice of notation for angles around faces does not go through directly through duality; also the primal and dual faces have opposite bipartite coloring. As a consequence, one has to be careful when reading the above duality relation on the right-hand-sides of the identities of Lemma
36
, which rely on our choice of notation around faces. We nevertheless have the following relations which can be derived from understanding the duality relation with our choice of notation, or by a direct computation using also Identity (
43
). More precisely, define the following which correspond to the right-hand-sides of Lemma
36
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{\,\square}_{(\alpha_{e},\beta_{e}),t}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{j+j\ell}\frac{\theta_{1,1}^{2}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})}{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}^{2}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})}\frac{\theta_{0,0}^{2}(t)}{\theta_{\ell,j}^{2}(t)}

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}_{(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{m}),t}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{m}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}(0)}{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i})}\frac{\theta_{0,0}(t+\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i})}{\theta_{0,0}(t)}

\displaystyle\mathsf{W}^{*}_{(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{m}),t}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{m}\prod_{i=1}^{m}\frac{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{i-1})}{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}(0)}\frac{\theta_{\ell,j}(t)}{\theta_{\ell,j}(t+\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{i-1})},

then, we have the following duality relations:

\begin{split}&\mathsf{W}^{\,\square}_{(\alpha_{e},\beta_{e}),t}=\frac{1}{\mathsf{W}^{\,\square}_{(\beta_{e},\alpha_{e}+\rho),t+\rho}}=\frac{1}{\mathsf{W}^{\,\square}_{(\tilde{\alpha}_{e},\tilde{\beta}_{e}),t+\rho}},\\
&\mathsf{W}_{(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{m}),t}=\frac{1}{\mathsf{W}^{*}_{(\alpha_{m},\dots,\alpha_{1}),t+\rho}},\ \mathsf{W}^{*}_{(\alpha_{1},\dots,\alpha_{m}),t}=\frac{1}{\mathsf{W}_{(\alpha_{m},\dots,\alpha_{1}),t+\rho}}.\end{split}

(46)

4.2
Equality of face-weights at square faces: Necessary Conditions III

Consider an Ising model on an infinite, isoradial graph
G
with real coupling constants
{\varepsilon}\mathsf{J}
, and the associated Ising-dimer model on the minimal graph
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
. Consider Fock’s dimer model on
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
with parameters
\tau,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t
satisfying Necessary Conditions II. The two models are gauge equivalent if and only if face-weights are equal at all faces. As a consequence, we must have equality of face-weights for every square face
y=\{e,e^{*}\}
, implying in particular that the face-weight at
y
must be negative. Using Equations (
13
) and (
40
), we thus have the following condition at every square face
y
:

-\sinh^{2}(2\mathsf{J}_{e})=\mathscr{W}^{\scriptsize{\mathrm{I}}}_{{\varepsilon}\mathsf{J}}(y)=\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(y,\tau)=(-1)^{j+j\ell}\frac{\theta_{1,1}^{2}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})}{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}^{2}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})}\frac{\theta_{0,0}^{2}(t)}{\theta_{\ell,j}^{2}(t)}<0,

(47)

where we use the notation of Figure
6
. In what follows, we identify parameters
\tau,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t
satisfying the negativity Condition (
47
).

Proposition 39
.

Consider Fock’s dimer model on an infinite, minimal graph
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
with parameters
\tau,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t
satisfying Necessary Conditions II. Then, if
\tau\in\frac{1}{2}+i{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
, the negativity Condition (
47
) is never satisfied.

Proof.

The proof consists in showing that the face-weight
\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(y,\tau)
is always positive.
Using Equation (
22
), we obtain

\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(y,\tau)=-\frac{\theta_{1,1}^{2}(\beta-\alpha)\theta_{0,0}^{2}(t)}{\theta_{1,0}^{2}(\beta-\alpha)\theta_{0,1}^{2}(t)}=\frac{\theta_{1,1}^{2}(\beta-\alpha)\theta_{1,0}^{2}(t+\tau/2)}{\theta_{1,0}^{2}(\beta-\alpha)\theta_{1,1}^{2}(t+\tau/2)}

Using the algebraic identity on theta functions from Lemma
75
of Appendix
A
, and denoting
\gamma=\beta-\alpha
,
s=t+\tau/2
and
\tau_{2}=2\tau-1
, this can be rewritten as

\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(y,\tau)=\frac{\theta_{1,1}^{2}(\gamma,\tau_{2})\theta_{0,0}^{2}(\gamma,\tau_{2})\theta_{1,0}^{2}(s,\tau_{2})\theta_{0,1}^{2}(s,\tau_{2})}{\theta_{1,0}^{2}(\gamma,\tau_{2})\theta_{0,1}^{2}(\gamma,\tau_{2})\theta_{1,1}^{2}(s,\tau_{2})\theta_{0,0}^{2}(s,\tau_{2})}

Note that since Necessary Conditions I are satisfied and
\tau\in 1/2+i{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
,
t\in\{\frac{1}{4},\frac{3}{4}\}+i{\mathbb{R}}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
so
s=t+\tau/2\in\{0,\frac{1}{2}\}+i{\mathbb{R}}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
.
Even though
\tau_{2}\in i{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
, the sign of
\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(y,\tau)
is not yet obvious from this equation since
\gamma,s\in\{0,\frac{1}{2}\}+i{\mathbb{R}}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
, and we only know that theta functions are real on
{\mathbb{R}}[\Lambda]
when the modulus is imaginary, so we need to use modular transformations.
If we let
j_{1},j_{2}\in\{0,1\}
be such that
\gamma+j_{1}/2,s+j_{2}/2\in i{\mathbb{R}}[\Lambda]
, by Equation (
22
)

\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(y,\tau)=\frac{\theta_{1,1+j_{1}}^{2}(\gamma+j_{1}/2,\tau_{2})\theta_{0,j_{1}}^{2}(\gamma+j_{1}/2,\tau_{2})\theta_{1,j_{2}}^{2}(s+j_{2}/2,\tau_{2})\theta_{0,1+j_{2}}^{2}(s+j_{2}/2,\tau_{2})}{\theta_{1,j_{1}}^{2}(\gamma+j_{1}/2,\tau_{2})\theta_{0,1+j_{1}}^{2}(\gamma+j_{1}/2,\tau_{2})\theta_{1,1+j_{2}}^{2}(s+j_{2}/2,\tau_{2})\theta_{0,j_{2}}^{2}(s+j_{2}/2,\tau_{2})}.

Using Equation (
26
) to change from imaginary arguments to real arguments modulo
[\Lambda]
, we see that this is positive real: all the constant factors from Equation (
26
) cancel and there remains only a product of squared theta functions of real arguments with pure imaginary modulus.
∎

Corollary 40
.

Consider an Ising-dimer model on an infinite, periodic, minimal graph
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
, and let
\mathscr{C}
be its spectral curve. Suppose that
\mathscr{C}
has genus 1 and satisfies assumptions
(\dagger)
. Then, the curve
\mathscr{C}
is maximal.

Proof.

Consider Fock’s gauge equivalent dimer model on
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}

[
32
]
, with parameters
\tau,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t
. Then, these parameters must satisfy Necessary Conditions II. Moreover, face-weights must be equal at all square faces which, by Proposition
39
implies that
\tau\neq\frac{1}{2}+i{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
. As a consequence, we have
\tau\in i{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
which, by Point 1. of Theorem
25
, implies maximality of the spectral curve
\mathscr{C}
.
∎

Suppose now that
\tau\in i{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
. Then, by Necessary Conditions I, the parameter
t
belongs to
{\mathbb{R}}+\bigl\{0,\frac{\tau}{2}\bigr\}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
and, for every square face
y=\{e,e^{*}\}
, the parameters
\alpha_{e},\beta_{e}
belong to
{\mathbb{R}}+\bigl\{0,\frac{\tau}{2}\bigr\}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
, so that we have:

t=\Re(t)+\frac{\ell_{t}}{2}\tau,\quad\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}=\Re(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})+\frac{\ell_{e}}{2}\tau,

(48)

for some
\ell_{t}\in\{0,1\}
, and
(\ell_{e})_{\scriptsize{\{y=\{e,e^{*}\}:\text{ square face}\}}}
taking values in
\{0,1\}
. By Necessary Condition II,
\rho=\frac{j}{2}+\frac{\ell}{2}\tau
, for some
(j,\ell)\neq(0,0)
.

Proposition 41
.

Suppose that
\tau\in i{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
, and consider Fock’s dimer model on an infinite, minimal graph
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
satisfying Necessary Conditions II, with parameters
\tau,\bm{\alpha},\rho=\frac{j}{2}+\frac{\ell}{2}\tau,t=\Re(t)+\frac{\ell_{t}}{2}\tau\in{\mathbb{R}}+\{0,\frac{\tau}{2}\}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
. Then,

1.

if
(j,\ell)=(0,1)
the negativity Condition (
47
) is never satisfied,

2.

if
(j,\ell)\in\{(1,0),(1,1)\}
, the negativity Condition (
47
) is satisfied if and only if, for every square face
y
,
\ell_{e}=(\ell_{t}+\ell)\ [2]
.
Moreover,

(a)

when
(j,\ell)=(1,0)
and
\ell_{t}=1
, the graph
G
and the dual graph
G^{*}
need to be bipartite,

(b)

when
(j,\ell)=(1,1)
and
\ell_{t}=0
, the dual graph
G^{*}
needs to be bipartite; and if
\ell_{t}=1
, the graph
G
needs to be bipartite.

Proof.

The proof consists in analyzing the condition
\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(y)<0
for every square face
y
. From Identity (
22
) we obtain, for every
z\in{\mathbb{C}}
,
s\in\{0,1\}
,

\theta_{m,n}\Bigl(z+\frac{s}{2}\tau\Bigr)=(q^{-\frac{1}{4}}e^{-i\pi z})^{s}(-i)^{ns}\theta_{m+s,n}(z)\quad\Rightarrow\quad\frac{\theta_{m,n}^{2}\bigl(z+\frac{s}{2}\tau\bigr)}{\theta_{m+\ell,n+j}^{2}(z+\frac{s}{2}\tau)}=(-1)^{js}\frac{\theta_{m+s,n}^{2}(z)}{\theta_{m+\ell+s,n+j}^{2}(z)}.

Therefore, setting
\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}=\Re(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})+\frac{\ell_{e}}{2}\tau
, and
t=\Re(t)+\frac{\ell_{t}}{2}\tau
in Equation (
40
) and using Identity (
22
), we obtain, for every square face
y
,

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(y)

\displaystyle=(-1)^{j+j\ell}(-1)^{j\ell_{e}}(-1)^{j\ell_{t}}\frac{\theta_{1+\ell_{e},1}^{2}(\Re(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}))}{\theta_{1+\ell+\ell_{e},1+j}^{2}(\Re(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}))}\frac{\theta_{\ell_{t},0}^{2}(\Re(t))}{\theta_{\ell+\ell_{t},j}^{2}(\Re(t))}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{j(\ell+1+\ell_{e}+\ell_{t})}\frac{\theta_{1+\ell_{e},1}^{2}(\Re(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}))}{\theta_{1+\ell+\ell_{e},1+j}^{2}(\Re(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}))}\frac{\theta_{\ell_{t},0}^{2}(\Re(t))}{\theta_{\ell+\ell_{t},j}^{2}(\Re(t))}.

(49)

Since
\tau\in i{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
, theta functions are real for real values of the parameters, so that the terms involving theta functions are positive, and we are left with considering the sign in front.

When
(j,\ell)=(0,1)
, the sign is always positive; when
(j,\ell)=(1,0)
, it is negative if and only if
\ell_{e}=\ell_{t}\ [2]
; when
(j,\ell)=(1,1)
, it is negative if and only if
\ell_{e}=(\ell_{t}+1)\ [2]
, thus proving the first part of Proposition
41
.

Now, consider a face of
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
corresponding to a dual vertex
f
of
G
. Using the notation of Figure
5
(left) we have, for every
i\in\{1,\dots,|f|\}
,
\beta_{i}-\alpha_{i}=\Re(\beta_{i}-\alpha_{i})+\frac{\ell_{i}}{2}\tau
, and using the first part of the statement:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{|f|}(\beta_{i}-\alpha_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{|f|}(\Re(\beta_{i}-\alpha_{i}))+\frac{\tau}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{|f|}\ell_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{|f|}(\Re(\beta_{i}-\alpha_{i}))+\frac{\tau}{2}\bigl(|f|(\ell_{t}+\ell)\ [2]\bigr)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{|f|}(\Re(\beta_{i}-\alpha_{i}))+\tau\Bigl(\frac{|f|}{2}(\ell_{t}+\ell)\ [1]\Bigr).

(50)

Moreover, by Equation (
38
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{|f|}(\beta_{i}-\alpha_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{|f|}(\alpha_{i-1}+\rho-\alpha_{i})=|f|\rho=\frac{|f|}{2}j+\tau\frac{|f|}{2}\ell.

(51)

Right-hand-sides of (
50
) and (
51
) need to be equal in
{\mathbb{T}}(\tau)
, implying that their imaginary parts need to be equal in
{\mathbb{T}}(\tau)
. Therefore, we must have

\frac{|f|}{2}(\ell_{t}+\ell)\,[1]=\frac{|f|}{2}\ell\ [1]
,
i.e.
,

\frac{|f|}{2}\ell_{t}\ [1]=0
.
This is true if
\ell_{t}=0
, but if
\ell_{t}=1
, this is true if and only
if
|f|
is even. Since the argument holds for every dual vertex
f
, we must have that all dual vertices have even degree, which is equivalent to saying that the graph
G
is bipartite.

Consider a face of
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
corresponding to a vertex
v
of
G
. Using the notation of Figure
5
(right), for every
i\in\{1,\dots,|v|\}
, we have the following relation between the angles using the primal or dual perspective:
\tilde{\beta}_{i}-\tilde{\alpha}_{i}=\alpha_{i}-\beta_{i}+\rho
. Using the first part of the statement, we thus have:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{|v|}(\tilde{\beta}_{i}-\tilde{\alpha}_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{|v|}(\alpha_{i}-\beta_{i}+\rho)=\sum_{i=1}^{|v|}(\Re(\alpha_{i}-\beta_{i}))+\frac{\tau}{2}(|v|(\ell_{t}+\ell)\ [2])+|v|\rho

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{|v|}(\Re(\alpha_{i}-\beta_{i}))+\frac{|v|}{2}j+\tau\Bigl(\frac{|v|}{2}(\ell_{t}+\ell)\ [1]+\frac{|v|}{2}\ell\Bigr).

Moreover, by Equation (
38
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{|v|}(\tilde{\beta}_{i}-\tilde{\alpha}_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{|v|}(\tilde{\alpha}_{i-1}+\rho-\tilde{\alpha}_{i})=|v|\rho=\frac{|v|}{2}j+\tau\frac{|v|}{2}\ell.

Again, we need the imaginary parts of both right-hand-sides to be equal in
{\mathbb{T}}(\tau)
, so that we must have that
\frac{|v|}{2}(\ell_{t}+\ell)=0\ [1]
. When
\ell_{t}=0
and
\ell=0
, or
\ell_{t}=1
and
\ell=1
, this is always true. When
\ell_{t}=1
and
\ell=0
, or
\ell_{t}=0
and
\ell=1
, this is true if and only if
|v|
is even. Since this holds at every primal vertex
v
, we deduce that the dual graph
G^{*}
is bipartite. Gathering these conditions proves the second part of the statement.
∎

Proposition
41
identifies the set of parameters
\bm{\alpha},\rho,t
of Fock’s dimer models satisfying the negativity Condition (
48
), but the condition on the angle map is given locally, that is for every square face
y=\{e,e^{*}\}
. This raises the question of the existence and characterization of (global) angle maps
\bm{\alpha}
satisfying these local constraints.

In order to handle this question we need to add additional information on the set of train-tracks
\vec{\mathscr{T}}
when either
G
or
G^{*}
is bipartite. Recall from Section
4.1.1
that, in general, there is no natural way of assigning labels
\vec{T}
and

\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle T\mkern 3.0mu$}}

to the two oriented train-tracks of
\vec{\mathscr{T}}
corresponding to a train-track
T
of
\mathscr{T}
just by looking at
T
. The situation is nevertheless different when the graph
G
or its dual graph
G^{*}
is bipartite. Suppose first that
G^{*}
is bipartite. Then the train-tracks
\mathscr{T}
of
G^{*}
can themselves be oriented so that white vertices of
G^{*}
are on the left. The oriented train-tracks
\vec{\mathscr{T}}
of
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
can be partitioned as
\vec{\mathscr{T}}=\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{*,\circ}\sqcup\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{*,\bullet}
, where
\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{*,\circ}
, resp.
\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{*,\bullet}
, consists of the train-tracks turning counterclockwise around every white, resp. black vertex of
G^{*}
; moreover the set
\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{*,\circ}
is in orientation preserving bijection with
\mathscr{T}
, see Figure
7
.

\begin{overpic}[fig_G_dual_bip_1.pdf]
\end{overpic}

Figure 7
:
Left: the oriented train-tracks of
\mathscr{T}
when
G
is the triangular lattice and
G^{*}
is the hexagonal lattice, which is bipartite. Right: the oriented train-tracks of
\vec{\mathscr{T}}
split into
\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{\circ,*}
(full lines, in orientation preserving bijection with
\mathscr{T}
), and
\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{\bullet,*}
(dotted lines).

When the graph
G
is bipartite, a similar construction can be done, with the word “face” replaced by “vertex”. This yields a partition of the set of oriented train-tracks
\vec{\mathscr{T}}
as
\vec{\mathscr{T}}=\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{\circ}\sqcup\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{\bullet}
, where
\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{\circ}
, resp.

\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{\bullet}
, consists of the train-tracks turning counterclockwise around every white, resp. black vertex of
G
. For every train-track
T
of
\mathscr{T}
, we then set the labeling
\vec{T}
,

\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle T\mkern 3.0mu$}}

as follows:

\text{If $G^{*}$ is bipartite, then }\vec{T}\in\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{*,\circ},\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle T\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle T\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle T\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle T\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}\in\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{*,\bullet};\quad\text{if $G$ is bipartite, then }\vec{T}\in\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{\circ},\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle T\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle T\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle T\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle T\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}\in\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{\bullet}.

(52)

Note that if both graphs
G
and
G^{*}
are bipartite, the above condition fixes the bipartite coloring of
G^{*}
once the coloring of
G
is set. Note also that only differences of angles occur in face-weights, so that we need to fix a base point for the angle map
\bm{\alpha}
. If
G
is not bipartite, we fix any
\vec{T}_{0}
, and set
\alpha_{\vec{T}_{0}}=0
(observing that this is compatible with Necessary Conditions I). If
G
, resp.
G^{*}
, is bipartite, then
\vec{T}_{0}
is fixed to belong to
\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{\circ}
, resp.
\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{*,\circ}
, and we also set
\alpha_{\vec{T}_{0}}=0
.

Definition 42
.

Consider an infinite, isoradial graph
G
, and the corresponding minimal graph
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
. Suppose that, up to modular transformations,
\tau\in i{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
, and consider parameters
\bm{\alpha},\rho=\frac{j}{2}+\frac{\ell}{2}\tau,t=\Re(t)+\frac{\ell_{t}}{2}\tau
satisfying Necessary Conditions II, recalling that Necessary Conditions II implies that, for every train-track
T
of
\mathscr{T}
,
\alpha_{\vec{T}}=\alpha_{\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle T\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle T\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle T\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle T\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}}+\rho\ [\Lambda]
.
Let us define the following sets
\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}
for the angle map
\bm{\alpha}
:

1.

Suppose that
(j,\ell)=(1,0),\ell_{t}=0
. For every train-track
T
of
\mathscr{T}
, choose any labeling
\vec{T}
and

\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle T\mkern 3.0mu$}}

for the two corresponding oriented train-tracks, and define:

\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}=\{\bm{\alpha}:\ \forall\,T\in\mathscr{T},\ \alpha_{\vec{T}}\in{\mathbb{R}}\penalty\ [\Lambda]\}.

Since
\rho=\frac{1}{2}
is real we have, for every
T\in\mathscr{T}
,
\alpha_{\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle T\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle T\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle T\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle T\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}}\in{\mathbb{R}}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
, so that the angle map
\bm{\alpha}
takes values in
{\mathbb{R}}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
.

2.

Suppose that
(j,\ell)=(1,0),\ell_{t}=1
. By Proposition
41
, both the graph
G
and the dual graph
G^{*}
are bipartite. We choose the labeling of the train-tracks as in Equation (
52
) (right), and define:

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}=\left\{\bm{\alpha}:\ \right.

\displaystyle\text{ around every primal and dual face, }\alpha_{\vec{T}}\text{ alternatively belongs to }

\displaystyle\left.{\mathbb{R}}\penalty\ [\Lambda]\text{ and }{\mathbb{R}}+\Bigl\{\frac{\tau}{2}\Bigr\}\penalty\ [\Lambda]\right\}.

This angle map can consistently be constructed, because both
G
and
G^{*}
are bipartite. Since
\rho=\frac{1}{2}
,
\alpha_{\vec{T}}
and
\alpha_{\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle T\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle T\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle T\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle T\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}}
both belong to the same component of the torus
{\mathbb{T}}(\tau)
.

3.

Suppose that
(j,\ell)=(1,1)
. By Proposition
41
, if
\ell_{t}=0
the dual graph
G^{*}
is bipartite, and if
\ell_{t}=1
the graph
G
is bipartite.
We choose the labeling of the pairs of train-tracks as in Equation (
52
) and define,

\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}=\{\bm{\alpha}:\ \forall\,T\in\mathscr{T},\ \alpha_{\vec{T}}\in{\mathbb{R}}\penalty\ [\Lambda]\}.

where recall that, for every
T\in\mathscr{T}
, in the first case we have
\vec{T}\in\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{*,\circ}
, while in the second
\vec{T}\in\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{\circ}
. Since
\rho
has imaginary part
\frac{\tau}{2}
we have, for every
T\in\mathscr{T}
,
\alpha_{\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle T\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle T\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle T\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle T\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}}\in{\mathbb{R}}+\{\frac{\tau}{2}\}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
.

Note that the set of angles
\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}
only depends on the imaginary part
\frac{\ell_{t}}{2}\tau
of
t
, and not on its real part.
Note also that the angle map
\bm{\alpha}
is generic since, for every train-track
T
of
\mathscr{T}
, there is no additional constraint on the angle
\alpha_{\vec{T}}
except from that of belonging to one of the real components of the torus
{\mathbb{T}}(\tau)
.

Proposition 43
.

Under the assumptions of Definition
42
, for
(j,\ell)=(1,0)
and
\ell_{t}\in\{0,1\}
, or
(j,\ell)=(1,1)
and
\ell_{t}\in\{0,1\}
, the angle map
\bm{\alpha}
satisfies the local condition:

\forall\,\text{square face $y=\{e,e^{*}\}$},\quad\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}=\Re(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})+\frac{\ell_{e}}{2}\tau,\ \text{ with $\ell_{e}=(\ell_{t}+\ell)\ [2]$},

(53)

if and only if
\bm{\alpha}
belongs to
\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}
.

Proof.

\bullet
Case
(j,\ell)=(1,0)
and
\ell_{t}=0
. Suppose that
\bm{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}
, then
since
\rho=\frac{1}{2}
is real, the angle map
\bm{\alpha}
takes values in
{\mathbb{R}}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
.
As a consequence, for every square face
y=\{e,e^{*}\}
,
\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}\in{\mathbb{R}}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
; it is thus of the form
\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}=\Re(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})+\frac{\ell_{e}}{2}\tau
, with
\ell_{e}=\ell_{t}+\ell\ [2]=0\ [2]
. Suppose now that
\bm{\alpha}\notin\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}
. Then, since the graph of train-tracks
\mathsf{G}^{\diamond}
is connected, since
\bm{\alpha}
satisfies Necessary Conditions I, since
\rho
is real, there exists two intersecting train-tracks
\vec{T}_{1}
,
\vec{T}_{2}
, such that
\alpha_{\vec{T}_{1}}\in{\mathbb{R}}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
,
and
\alpha_{\vec{T}_{2}}\in{\mathbb{R}}+\{\frac{\tau}{2}\}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
. Since the train-tracks
\vec{T}_{1}
,
\vec{T}_{2}
intersect, they must do so at a square face
y
, implying that we either have
\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}
or
\tilde{\beta}_{e}-\tilde{\alpha}_{e}
that belongs to

{\mathbb{R}}+\{\frac{\tau}{2}\}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
. But, by Necessary Conditions II,
\tilde{\beta}_{e}-\tilde{\alpha}_{e}=\alpha_{e}-\beta_{e}+\rho\ [\Lambda]=\alpha_{e}-\beta_{e}+\frac{1}{2}\ [\Lambda]
, so that both
\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}
and
\tilde{\beta}_{e}-\tilde{\alpha}_{e}
must belong to

{\mathbb{R}}+\{\frac{\tau}{2}\}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
, and in particular
\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}
. This means that at the square face
y
, Condition (
53
) is not satisfied, ending the proof of this case. The case
(i,j)=(1,0)
and
\ell_{t}1
is treated below.

\bullet
Case
(j,\ell)=(1,1)
and
\ell_{t}=0
. Note that because of the choice of labeling for pairs of train-tracks when the graph
G^{*}
is bipartite, for every square
y=\{e,e^{*}\}
, we either have
\alpha_{e}\in\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{*,\circ}
and
\beta_{e}\in\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{*,\bullet}
, or
\alpha_{e}\in\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{*,\bullet}
and
\beta_{e}\in\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{*,\circ}
; we also have that
\tilde{\alpha}_{e},\tilde{\beta}_{e}
either both belong to
\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{*,\circ}
, or they both belong to
\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{*,\bullet}
.
Suppose that
\bm{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}
, then because of the labeling of pairs of train-tracks, we have that,
for every square face
y
,
\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}\in{\mathbb{R}}+\{\frac{\tau}{2}\}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
;
it is thus of the form
\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}=\Re(\beta-\alpha)+\frac{\ell_{e}}{2}\tau
, with
\ell_{e}=\ell_{t}+\ell\ [2]=1\ [2]
.
Suppose now that
\bm{\alpha}\notin\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}
. Then, since the graph of train-tracks
\mathsf{G}^{\diamond\scriptscriptstyle{/2}}
is connected, since
\bm{\alpha}
satisfies Necessary Conditions I, there exists two train-tracks
\vec{T}_{1},\vec{T}_{2}\in\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{\circ}
such that

\alpha_{\vec{T}_{1}}\in{\mathbb{R}}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
, and
\alpha_{\vec{T}_{2}}\in{\mathbb{R}}+\{\frac{\tau}{2}\}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
, intersecting at a square face
y
. Because of our choice of labeling for pairs of train-tracks, they must correspond to and edge crossed by train-tracks with angles
\tilde{\alpha}_{e},\tilde{\beta}_{e}
.
We thus have
\tilde{\beta}_{e}-\tilde{\alpha}_{e}\in{\mathbb{R}}+\{\frac{\tau}{2}\}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
which, with the condition
\tilde{\beta}_{e}-\tilde{\alpha}_{e}=\alpha_{e}-\beta_{e}+\rho\ [\Lambda]=\alpha_{e}-\beta_{e}+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{2}\tau\ [\Lambda]
,
implies that
\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}\in{\mathbb{R}}/{\mathbb{Z}}
. This means that at the square face
y
, Condition (
53
) is not satisfied, ending the proof of this case.

\bullet
Case
(j,\ell)=(1,1)
and
\ell_{t}=1
. The proof is similar to the case
(j,\ell)=(1,1)
and
\ell_{t}=0
, with the role of
\alpha_{e},\beta_{e}
taken by
\tilde{\alpha}_{e},\tilde{\beta}_{e}
. Because of the choice of labeling for pairs of train-tracks when
G
is bipartite, for every square
y
, we have that either
\alpha_{e},\beta_{e}
both belong to
\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{\circ}
, or they both belong to
\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{\bullet}
; we also have that

\tilde{\alpha}_{e}\in\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{\circ}
and
\tilde{\beta}_{e}\in\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{\bullet}
, or
\tilde{\alpha}_{e}\in\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{\bullet}
and
\tilde{\beta}_{e}\in\vec{\mathscr{T}}_{\circ}
. The remainder of the argument is so similar that we do not repeat it.

\bullet
Case
(j,\ell)=(1,0)
and
\ell_{t}=1
. Suppose that
\bm{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}
. Then, by definition of the angle map, and since
\alpha_{\vec{T}}
and
\alpha_{\mathchoice{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\displaystyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\displaystyle T\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 3.0mu\reflectbox{$\textstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-3.0mu\textstyle T\mkern 3.0mu$}}$}\mkern-3.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptstyle T\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}{\mkern 2.0mu\reflectbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\vec{\reflectbox{$\mkern-2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle T\mkern 2.0mu$}}$}\mkern-2.0mu}}
belong to the same real component we have that, for every square face
y=\{e,e^{*}\}
,

\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}\in{\mathbb{R}}+\{\frac{\tau}{2}\}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
; it is thus of the form
\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}=\mathscr{R}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})+\frac{\ell_{e}}{2}\tau
, with
\ell_{e}=\ell_{t}+\ell\ [2]=1\ [2]
. The remainder of the argument is a combination of the above, and we do not repeat it here.
∎

Let us now wrap up. Consider an Ising model on an infinite, isoradial graph
G
with real coupling constants
{\varepsilon}\mathsf{J}
, and the corresponding Ising-dimer model on
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
. Consider Fock’s dimer model on
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
with parameters
\tau,\bm{\alpha},\rho=\frac{j}{2}+\frac{\ell}{2}\tau,t=\Re(t)+\frac{\ell_{t}}{2}\tau
satisfying Necessary Conditions II. Then, if the two models are gauge equivalent, by Proposition
41
, the modular parameter
\tau
must belong to
i{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
, the case
(j,\ell)=(0,1)
cannot occur, and angles of the angle map
\bm{\alpha}
need to satisfy a local condition at every square face. The local condition on the angle map
\bm{\alpha}
is proved to be equivalent to a global condition in Proposition
43
, it also requires some additional bipartitedness assumptions on the underlying graphs. This motivates the following.

Definition 44
.

We say that the parameters
\tau,\bm{\alpha},\rho=\frac{j}{2}+\frac{\ell}{2}\tau,t=\Re(t)+\frac{\ell_{t}}{2}\tau
of Fock’s dimer model on the graph
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
satisfy
Necessary Conditions III
if they satisfy Necessary Conditions II and if, up to modular transformations of
\tau
,

\bullet

\tau\in i{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
;
(j,\ell)\neq(0,1)
; and the angle map
\bm{\alpha}
belongs to the set
\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}
of Definition
42
;

Furthermore,

\bullet

when
\rho=\frac{1}{2}
and
\ell_{t}=1
, the graph
G
and the dual graph
G^{*}
are bipartite;

\bullet

when
\rho=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2}
and
\ell_{t}=0
, the dual graph
G^{*}
is bipartite, and when
\ell_{t}=1
, the graph
G
is bipartite.

When the parameters
\tau,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t
satisfy Necessary Conditions III, using Equation (
49
) and Proposition
41
(in particular the fact that
j=1
), we can rewrite Condition (
47
) as:

-\sinh^{2}(2\mathsf{J}_{e})=(-1)^{j+j\ell}\frac{\theta_{1,1}^{2}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})}{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}^{2}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})}\frac{\theta_{0,0}^{2}(t)}{\theta_{\ell,j}^{2}(t)}=-\frac{\theta_{1+\ell_{t}+\ell,1}^{2}(\Re(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}))}{\theta_{1+\ell_{t},0}^{2}(\Re(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}))}\frac{\theta_{\ell_{t},0}^{2}(\Re(t))}{\theta_{\ell+\ell_{t},1}^{2}(\Re(t))}<0.

(54)

This allows us to determine the absolute value coupling constants
\mathsf{J}
for every square face
y=\{e,e^{*}\}
; let us denote by
\mathsf{J}_{e}=\mathsf{J}_{e,(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)}
the solution to Equation (
54
).

Remark 45
.

On the dual graph
G^{*}
, define the absolute value
\mathsf{J}^{*}_{e^{*},(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)}
to be the solution of

\sinh^{2}(2\mathsf{J}^{*}_{e^{*},(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)})=-\frac{1}{\mathscr{W}_{G,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}}(y).

Let
{\varepsilon}^{*}
denote any sign function on the edges of the dual graph.
Then using subsequently: the definition of Ising-dimer weights, the definition of
\mathsf{J}^{*}_{e^{*},(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)}
, and Condition (
47
), we obtain

\mathscr{W}^{\scriptsize{\mathrm{I}}}_{G^{*},{\varepsilon}^{*}\mathsf{J}^{*}_{(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)}}(y)=-\sinh^{-2}(2\mathsf{J}^{*}_{e^{*},(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)})=\mathscr{W}_{G,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(y)=\mathscr{W}^{\scriptsize{\mathrm{I}}}_{G,{\varepsilon}\mathsf{J}_{(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)}}(y),

so that, recalling Definition
13
, the coupling constants
{\varepsilon}^{*}\mathsf{J}^{*}_{(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)}
are the weak dual of the coupling constant
{\varepsilon}\mathsf{J}_{(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)}
.
Note that the signs
{\varepsilon}^{*}
do not matter here since weak duality only involves the absolute values of the coupling constants.
Note also that by Lemma
37
we have:

\mathscr{W}^{\scriptsize{\mathrm{I}}}_{G^{*},{\varepsilon}^{*}\mathsf{J}^{*}_{(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)}}(y)=\mathscr{W}_{G^{*},\bm{\alpha},\rho,t+\rho}(y).

As a consequence, we say that Fock’s dimer model on
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
with parameters
\tau,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t
and parameters
\tau,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t+\rho
are
weakly dual
.

4.3
Equality of squared face-weights: Necessary Conditions IV

Consider an Ising model on an infinite, periodic, isoradial graph
G
with periodic, real coupling constants
{\varepsilon}\mathsf{J}
, and the corresponding Ising-dimer model on the minimal graph
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
. Consider Fock’s dimer model with parameters
\tau,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t
. Suppose that the two models are gauge equivalent, implying that the parameters
\tau,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t
satisfy Necessary Conditions III, and recall that Necessary Conditions III allows to define the absolute value coupling constants
\mathsf{J}_{(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)}
, see Equation (
54
). If the two models are gauge equivalent, we must also have equality of the squared face-weights at all faces of
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
corresponding to dual vertices, resp. primal vertices, namely:

\forall\,f\in V^{*},\quad(\mathscr{W}^{\scriptsize{\mathrm{I}}}_{{\varepsilon}\mathsf{J}_{(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)}})^{2}(f)=(\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t})^{2}(f),\forall\,v\in V,\quad(\mathscr{W}^{\scriptsize{\mathrm{I}}}_{{\varepsilon}\mathsf{J}_{(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)}})^{2}(v)=(\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t})^{2}(v).

(55)

4.3.1
Necessary Conditions IV

In the following, we identify conditions on the parameters
\rho
and
t
so that Equation (
55
) is satisfied whenever Equation (
54
) is.

Proposition 46
.

Consider Fock’s dimer model on an infinite, minimal graph
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
with parameters
\tau,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t
satisfying Necessary Conditions III,
i.e.
,
\rho=\frac{j}{2}+\frac{\ell}{2}\tau
,
t=\Re(t)+\frac{\ell_{t}}{2}\tau
, with
(j,\ell)\in\{(1,0),(1,1)\}
, and
\ell_{t}\in\{0,1\}
.
For every
\bm{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\ell_{t}/2}
consider the absolute value coupling constants
\mathsf{J}_{(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)}

of Equation (
54
). Then,
Condition (
55
) is satisfied for every generic angle map
\bm{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}
if and only if,

1.

either
\rho=\frac{1}{2}
and
t\in\{0,\frac{1}{2}\}
,

2.

or
\rho=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2}
and
t\in\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{\tau}{2}\}
.

In particular, if the model is non degenerate, Condition (
55
) is never satisfied when
\rho=\frac{1}{2}
and
\ell_{t}=1
.

Proof.

Fix
\rho,t
as above. Consider
\bm{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}
and the absolute value coupling constants
\mathsf{J}_{(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)}
given by Equation (
54
). Condition (
54
) is satisfied at every square face, and we have,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tanh^{2}(2\mathsf{J}_{e,(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)})}

\displaystyle=1+\frac{1}{\sinh^{2}(2\mathsf{J}_{e,(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)})}=1-(-1)^{j+j\ell}\frac{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}^{2}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})\theta_{\ell,j}^{2}(t)}{\theta_{1,1}^{2}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})\theta_{0,0}^{2}(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\theta_{1,1}^{2}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})\theta_{0,0}^{2}(t)-(-1)^{j+j\ell}\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}^{2}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})\theta_{\ell,j}^{2}(t)}{\theta_{1,1}^{2}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})\theta_{0,0}^{2}(t)}

\displaystyle=(-1)^{\ell+j\ell}\frac{\theta_{\ell,j}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}+t)\theta_{\ell,j}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e}-t)\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}^{2}(0)}{\theta_{1,1}^{2}(\beta_{e}-\alpha_{e})\theta_{0,0}^{2}(t)},

where in the last line, we used Identity (
24
) at
(m,n)=(1+\ell,1+j)
.

Let
f
be a dual vertex of
G
with
|f|
boundary faces, and let
(e_{i})_{i=1,\dots,|f|}
denote its boundary edges, ordered counterclockwise. Then, using the notation of Figure
5
(left), Equation (
13
) at the face corresponding to
f
and the above identity for
\tanh^{-2}(2\mathsf{J}_{e_{i},(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)})
, we have

\displaystyle(\mathscr{W}^{\scriptsize{\mathrm{I}}}_{{\varepsilon}\mathsf{J}_{(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)}})^{2}(f)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}\frac{1}{\tanh^{2}(2\mathsf{J}_{e_{i},(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)})}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}(-1)^{\ell+j\ell}\frac{\theta_{\ell,j}(\beta_{i}-\alpha_{i}+t)\theta_{\ell,j}(\beta_{i}-\alpha_{i}-t)\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}^{2}(0)}{\theta_{1,1}^{2}(\beta_{i}-\alpha_{i})\theta_{0,0}^{2}(t)}

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}\frac{\theta_{0,0}(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i}+t)\theta_{0,0}(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i}-t)\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}^{2}(0)}{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}^{2}(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i})\theta_{0,0}^{2}(t)},

where in the last equality we used that, for every
i\in\{1,\dots,|f|\}
,
\beta_{i}-\alpha_{i}=\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i}+\rho
, and Identity (
22
).

On the other hand, by Equation (
41
) of Lemma
36
, we have

(\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t})^{2}(f)=\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}\frac{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}^{2}(0)}{\theta_{1+\ell,1+j}^{2}(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i})}\frac{\theta_{0,0}^{2}(t+\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i})}{\theta_{0,0}^{2}(t)}.

Therefore we have, for every generic
\bm{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}
, for every face corresponding to a dual face
f
,
(\mathscr{W}^{\scriptsize{\mathrm{I}}}_{{\varepsilon}\mathsf{J}_{(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)}})^{2}(f)=(\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t})^{2}(f)
if and only if, for every generic
\bm{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}
, for every
f
,

\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}\theta_{0,0}(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i}+t)=\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}\theta_{0,0}(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i}-t)\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}\frac{\theta_{0,0}(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i}+t)}{\theta_{0,0}(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i}-t)}=1.

Note that because of our choice of labeling, see Figure
5
, train-tracks associated to
\alpha_{i}
’s for distinct
i
’s do not belong to pairs of train-tracks around
f
. Moreover, since the graph
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
is minimal, they correspond to distinct train-tracks of
\mathscr{T}
, see
[
11
, Lemma 8]
.
By definition of
\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}
, this means that the
\alpha_{i}
’s for distinct values of
i
are generically independent and live on a real component of
{\mathbb{T}}(\tau)
.
The above condition thus implies that, for every
i\in\{1,\dots,|f|\}
, for every
\alpha_{i}
living on a real component of
{\mathbb{T}}(\tau)
,

\frac{\theta_{0,0}(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i}+t)}{\theta_{0,0}(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i}-t)}\frac{\theta_{0,0}(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{i+1}+t)}{\theta_{0,0}(\alpha_{i}-\alpha_{i+1}-t)}=c,

(56)

where the term
c
of the right-hand-side is constant when seen as a function of
\alpha_{i}
. By Lemma
19
, the left-hand-side is a meromorphic function of
\alpha_{i}
on
{\mathbb{T}}(\tau)
which needs to be equal to a constant on a real component of
{\mathbb{T}}(\tau)
, hence it must be equal to a constant on
{\mathbb{T}}(\tau)
. This is true if and only if the poles and the zeros cancel, and using that the
\alpha_{i}
’s for distinct
i
’s are generic, this is true if and only if

\displaystyle\alpha_{i-1}+t=\alpha_{i-1}-t\ [\Lambda],\text{ and }\alpha_{i+1}+t=\alpha_{i+1}-t\ [\Lambda]\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad t=-t\ [\Lambda]\quad\Leftrightarrow\quad

\displaystyle 2t=0\ [\Lambda].

When
\ell_{t}=0
,
t
is real, so that we must have
t\in\{0,\frac{1}{2}\}
. Recall further that by Lemma
36
, if the model is non degenerate,
t\notin\{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2},\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2}+\rho\}
. This implies that if
\rho=\frac{1}{2}
, then
t\in\{0,\frac{1}{2}\}
, and if
\rho=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2}
, then
t=\frac{1}{2}
.

When
\ell_{t}=1
,
t
has imaginary part equal to
\frac{\tau}{2}
, so that we must have
t\in\{\frac{\tau}{2},\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2}\}
. When
\rho=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2}
, the non-degeneration condition implies furthermore that
t=\frac{\tau}{2}
. When
\rho=\frac{1}{2}
, the non-degeneration condition implies that
t\notin\{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2},\frac{\tau}{2}\}
, so that in this case there exists no
t
such that the model is non-degenerate and satisfies Equation (
56
). This yields the first part of the result.

We thus assume that either
\rho=\frac{1}{2}
and
t\in\{0,\frac{1}{2}\}
, or
\rho=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2}
and
t\in\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{\tau}{2}\}
.
We know that the ratio (
56
) is constant, but we still need to check that
\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}\frac{\theta_{0,0}(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i}+t)}{\theta_{0,0}(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i}-t)}=1
.
The parameter
t
is of the form
t=\frac{j_{t}}{2}+\frac{\ell_{t}}{2}\tau
for some
j_{t}\in\{0,1\}
, so that
t=-t+j_{t}+\ell_{t}\tau
; note also that we have the condition
j_{t}\ell_{t}=0
. Using Identity (
20
) we have

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}\theta_{0,0}(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i}+t)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}\theta_{0,0}(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i}-t+j_{t}+\ell_{t}\tau)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}(q^{-1}e^{-2i\pi(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i}-t)})^{\ell_{t}}\theta_{0,0}(\alpha_{i-1}-\alpha_{i}-t).

The exponential term involving the angles
(\alpha_{i})
cancels because the latter are fixed. Moreover, recalling that
q=e^{i\pi\tau}
, we have
q^{-\ell_{t}}e^{-2i\pi\ell_{t}t}=e^{-i\pi\ell_{t}\tau}e^{i\pi(j_{t}\ell_{t}+\ell_{t}\tau)}=e^{i\pi j_{t}\ell_{t}}
. The latter is equal to 1 because
j_{t}\ell_{t}=0
, thus concluding the first part of the statement and the condition on equality of squared face-weights at faces corresponding to dual vertices
f
.

Fix
\rho,t
as above. Consider
\bm{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}
and the absolute value
{\varepsilon}^{*}\mathsf{J}^{*}_{(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)}
of the dual coupling constants of Remark
45
. Then, if Equation (
47
) is satisfied at every square face
y
, by Remark
45
we also have, for every
y
,

\mathscr{W}^{\scriptsize{\mathrm{I}}}_{G^{*},{\varepsilon}^{*}\mathsf{J}^{*}_{(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)}}(y)=\mathscr{W}_{G^{*},\bm{\alpha},\rho,t+\rho}(y).

Otherwise stated Condition (
47
) is also satisfied from the dual graph
G^{*}
perspective. Repeating the above argument from the dual perspective yields that, for every face corresponding to a primal vertex
v
(which now corresponds to a dual vertex of
G^{*}
),

(\mathscr{W}^{\scriptsize{\mathrm{I}}})^{2}_{G^{*},{\varepsilon}^{*}\mathsf{J}^{*}_{(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)}}(v)=(\mathscr{W})^{2}_{G^{*},\bm{\alpha},\rho,t+\rho}(v),

if and only if
2(t+\rho)=0\ [\Lambda]
,
i.e.

2t=0\ [\Lambda]
, since
2\rho=0\ [\Lambda]
. Now, using Equation (
15
) and Lemma
37
we obtain:

(\mathscr{W}^{\scriptsize{\mathrm{I}}})^{2}_{G,{\varepsilon}\mathsf{J}_{(\bm{\alpha},\rho,t)}}(v)=(\mathscr{W})^{2}_{G,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(v),

if and only if
t=0\ [\Lambda]
, thus proving equality of the squared face-weights at faces corresponding to primal vertices of
G
, and ending the proof.
∎

Remark 47
.

Note that the allowed values of
t
are derived from assuming genericity of the angle map
\bm{\alpha}
, but that if
t
is as given by Point 1. or Point 2., then Condition (
54
) is satisfied.

Proposition
46
, and Remark
47
motivate the following.

Definition 48
.

We say that the parameters
\tau,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t
of Fock’s dimer model on the infinite, minimal graph
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
satisfy
Necessary Conditions IV
if they satisfy Necessary Conditions III (implying in particular that
\tau\in i{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
up to modular transformations, and that the angle map
\bm{\alpha}
belongs to
\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}
), and if moreover:

1.

either
\rho=\frac{1}{2}
and
t\in\{0,\frac{1}{2}\}
,

2.

or
\rho=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2}
,
t=\frac{1}{2}
and the dual graph
G^{*}
is bipartite; or
\rho=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2}
,
t=\frac{\tau}{2}
and the graph
G
is bipartite.

4.3.2
Computation of face-weights using Jacobi elliptic functions

We now compute face-weights of Fock’s dimer model on the infinite, minimal graph
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
with parameters
\tau,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t
satisfying Necessary Conditions IV. It turns out that they have natural expressions using Jacobi elliptic functions, whose definition we now recall. Consider a modular parameter
\tau\in i{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
. The
elliptic modulus
and
complementary elliptic modulus
are defined as

k=k(\tau)=\frac{\theta_{1,0}^{2}(0,\tau)}{\theta_{0,0}^{2}(0,\tau)}\quad;\quad k^{\prime}=k^{\prime}(k)=k^{\prime}(\tau)=\frac{\theta_{0,1}^{2}(0,\tau)}{\theta_{0,0}^{2}(0,\tau)};

(57)

they satisfy
k^{2}+(k^{\prime})^{2}=1.

When
\tau\in i{\mathbb{R}}^{+}
, then
k^{2}
belongs to
[0,1)
and
(k^{\prime})^{2}
to
(0,1]
(we allow
\tau=i\infty
as a degenerate case but not
\tau=0
).

From now on, we thus write parameters of Fock’s dimer model as
k,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t
, with
k^{2}\in[0,1)
; the parameter
k
is considered up to modular transformations.

The
complete elliptic integral of the first kind
, denoted by
K(k)
, and its complementary, denoted by
K^{\prime}(k)
, are defined by:

K=K(k)=\int_{0}^{\pi/2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{1-k^{2}\sin^{2}x}}dx\quad;\quad K^{\prime}(k):=K(k^{\prime}),

see Equation 22.2.2 of
[
25
]
. They are related to the modular parameter by
\tau=i\frac{K^{\prime}(k)}{K(k)}
.

Jacobi elliptic functions
are periodic, meromorphic functions on
{\mathbb{C}}
characterized, up to normalization, by the position of their poles and zeros. There are twelve of them, and we only introduce those used in this paper. Introducing the notation
\alpha^{k}=2K(k)\alpha
, they are expressed using the following ratios of theta functions, see
[
56
, Section 2.1]
for example.

\begin{split}\sn(\alpha^{k}|k)&=-\frac{\theta_{0,0}(0,\tau)}{\theta_{1,0}(0,\tau)}\frac{\theta_{1,1}(\alpha,\tau)}{\theta_{0,1}(\alpha,\tau)},\quad\cn(\alpha^{k}|k)=\frac{\theta_{0,1}(0,\tau)}{\theta_{1,0}(0,\tau)}\frac{\theta_{1,0}(\alpha,\tau)}{\theta_{0,1}(\alpha,\tau)},\\
\dn(\alpha^{k}|k)&=\frac{\theta_{0,1}(0,\tau)}{\theta_{0,0}(0,\tau)}\frac{\theta_{0,0}(\alpha,\tau)}{\theta_{0,1}(\alpha,\tau)}.\end{split}

(58)

Whenever no confusion occurs, we omit the arguments
\tau
and
k
in the notation.
The other Jacobi elliptic functions are defined in terms of these three basic functions, for example

\begin{split}\sc(\alpha^{k})&=-\frac{\theta_{0,0}(0)}{\theta_{0,1}(0)}\frac{\theta_{1,1}(\alpha)}{\theta_{1,0}(\alpha)},\hskip 9.24994pt\dc(\alpha^{k})=\frac{\theta_{1,0}(0)}{\theta_{0,0}(0)}\frac{\theta_{0,0}(\alpha)}{\theta_{1,0}(\alpha)},\hskip 9.24994pt\sd(\alpha^{k})=-\frac{\theta_{0,0}^{2}(0)}{\theta_{0,1}(0)\theta_{1,0}(0)}\frac{\theta_{1,1}(\alpha)}{\theta_{0,0}(\alpha)}.\end{split}

(59)

Recall that our final goal is to control the sign of
\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(\,\cdot\,)
when computed at faces corresponding to primal and dual vertices. When
k^{2}\in[0,1)
, the sign of elliptic functions is well known when their arguments are real. Hence, in the following we express face-weights using real arguments. When no real part is taken, it means that the argument is real.
We use the notation introduced in Section
4.1.3
, see Figures
5
and
6
.

Lemma 49
.

Consider Fock’s dimer model on an infinite, minimal graph
{\mathsf{G}}^{\scriptscriptstyle{\mathrm{Q}}}
with parameters

k,\bm{\alpha},\rho,t
satisfying Necessary Conditions IV, implying in particular that
k^{2}\in[0,1)
up to modular transformations, and that
\bm{\alpha}
belongs to
\mathcal{A}_{\rho,\,\ell_{t}/2}
. Then,

\bullet

When
\rho=\frac{1}{2}
, and
t\in\{0,\frac{1}{2}\}
, every angle map
\bm{\alpha}
belonging to
\mathcal{A}_{\frac{1}{2},0}
takes values in
{\mathbb{R}}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
, and face-weights are explicitly given by:

\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|}\hline\cr\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(\,\cdot\,)&t=0&t=\frac{1}{2}\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr y&-\sc^{2}(\beta^{k}_{e}-\alpha^{k}_{e})&-(k^{\prime})^{2}\sc^{2}(\beta^{k}_{e}-\alpha^{k}_{e})\\
\hline\cr f&\displaystyle{(-1)^{|f|}\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}\ns(\beta^{k}_{i}-\alpha^{k}_{i})}&\displaystyle{(-1)^{|f|}\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}\frac{1}{k^{\prime}}\ds(\beta^{k}_{i}-\alpha^{k}_{i})}\\
\hline\cr v&\displaystyle{(-1)^{|v|}\prod_{i=1}^{|v|}k^{\prime}\sd(\tilde{\beta}^{k}_{i}-\tilde{\alpha}^{k}_{i})}&\displaystyle{(-1)^{|v|}\prod_{i=1}^{|v|}\sn(\tilde{\beta}^{k}_{i}-\tilde{\alpha}^{k}_{i})}\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

(60)

\bullet

When
\rho=\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2}
, and
t=\frac{1}{2}
, the dual graph
G^{*}
is bipartite. For every angle map
\bm{\alpha}\in\mathcal{A}_{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\tau}{2},0}

at faces corresponding to dual vertices belong to
{\mathbb{R}}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
, and the differences of angles
(\tilde{\beta}_{i}-\tilde{\alpha}_{i})
at faces corresponding to primal vertices belong to
{\mathbb{R}}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
.
When

, and

belong to

{\mathbb{R}}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
, and the differences of angles

belong to
{\mathbb{R}}\penalty\ [\Lambda]
.

\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|}\hline\cr\mathscr{W}_{\bm{\alpha},\rho,t}(\,\cdot\,)&t=\frac{1}{2},\text{ $G^{*}$ bipartite }&t=\frac{\tau}{2},\text{ $G$ bipartite }\\
\hline\cr\hline\cr y&-\displaystyle{\frac{(k^{\prime})^{2}}{k^{2}}\nc^{2}(\mathscr{R}(\beta^{k}_{e}-\alpha^{k}_{e}))}&-k^{2}\sd^{2}(\mathscr{R}(\beta^{k}_{e}-\alpha^{k}_{e}))\\
\hline\cr f&\displaystyle{(-1)^{|f|}\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}\nd(\alpha^{k}_{i-1}-\alpha^{k}_{i})}&\displaystyle{(-1)^{|f|}\prod_{i=1}^{|f|}\frac{1}{k}\ns(\beta^{k}_{i}-\alpha^{k}_{i})}\\
\hline\cr v&\displaystyle{(-1)^{|v|}\prod_{i=1}^{|v|}k\sn(\tilde{\beta}^{k}_{i}-\tilde{\alpha}^{k}_{i})}&(-1)^{|v|}\prod_{i=1}^{|v|}\dn(\tilde{\alpha}^{k}_{i}-\tilde{\alpha}^{k}_{i-1})\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

(61)

Proof.

\bullet
